### Task 9: `--render` on `council verdict` + `render:true` on `amicus_verdict`

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/cli-handlers-council.js` (EXISTING — `runVerdict` gains `--render`)
- Modify: `src/mcp-server.js` (grandfathered — `amicus_verdict` `render:true` branch)
- Modify: `src/mcp-tools.js` (grandfathered — `amicus_verdict` inputSchema += `render`, `outDir`; annotations `readOnlyHint: true → false` + description reworded — the tool can now write `report.html`)
- Modify: `src/cli.js` (grandfathered — `--render` usage line under `council verdict`; **Task 9 owns that usage line** — Task 8 only registers `render` in `isBooleanFlag` and adds the `run` flag lines)
- Test: `tests/cli-council-verdict-render.test.js`, `tests/mcp-verdict-render.test.js` (Create)
- Test: `tests/mcp-tools.test.js` (Modify — the existing `readOnlyHint: true` pin covering `amicus_verdict` flips to `false`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `buildVerdict`/`writeVerdictAtomic` (C9), `buildReport` (C10), `fenceSidecarOutput` (C13).
- Produces: per the Shared-contracts `--render` block. CLI: `council verdict … -o <out.json> --render` writes `verdict.json` then `buildReport({verdict},{format:'html'})` → `<dir(out)>/report.html` (the decided verdict replaces the engine's undecided one and refreshes the html in place). The pinned command tail is unchanged — `--render` is appended after `-o` (Ambiguity 6), so the B22 regex still matches. MCP: `amicus_verdict {render:true, outDir:'<abs>'}` returns the `--md` rendering in the fenced tool result AND writes `<outDir>/report.html` — `outDir` is resolved against the project dir and must stay inside it (`isPathInside`, the same containment guard `amicus_council_run` applies at `src/mcp-council-run.js:93`), else the call returns `isError` and writes nothing; without `outDir`, returns md, writes nothing.

```js
// tests/cli-council-verdict-render.test.js
'use strict';
const fs = require('fs');
const os = require('os');
const path = require('path');
const { handleCouncil } = require('../src/cli-handlers-council');

function tallyDoc() {
  return {
    schemaVersion: 2, type: 'council-tally',
    meta: { runId: 'r', runType: 'headless', date: 'd', chair: 'deepseek', models: ['gemini', 'gpt'], claudeInCouncil: false },
    findings: [{ id: 'A1', raiser: 'gemini', severity: 'major', tier: 'Confirmed', basis: { a: 2, d: 0, n: 0 },
      confidence: 'solid', tierOverride: null, adjudications: [] }],
    rankings: [], streetCred: [], runStats: [], tierCounts: { Confirmed: 1, Contested: 0, Singleton: 0, Disputed: 0 }, judged: true,
  };
}

test('council verdict --render writes verdict.json AND report.html next to it', async () => {
  const dir = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'render-'));
  const tallyPath = path.join(dir, 'tally.json');
  fs.writeFileSync(tallyPath, JSON.stringify(tallyDoc()));
  const outPath = path.join(dir, 'verdict.json');
  const code = await handleCouncil({ _: ['council', 'verdict', tallyPath], out: outPath, render: true });
  expect(code).toBe(0);
  expect(fs.existsSync(outPath)).toBe(true);
  expect(fs.existsSync(path.join(dir, 'report.html'))).toBe(true);
  expect(fs.readFileSync(path.join(dir, 'report.html'), 'utf-8')).toContain('Council Report');
});

  const dir = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'norender-'));
  const tallyPath = path.join(dir, 'tally.json');
  fs.writeFileSync(tallyPath, JSON.stringify(tallyDoc()));
  const outPath = path.join(dir, 'verdict.json');
  await handleCouncil({ _: ['council', 'verdict', tallyPath], out: outPath });
  expect(fs.existsSync(path.join(dir, 'report.html'))).toBe(false);
});
```

Create `tests/mcp-verdict-render.test.js`:

```js
// tests/mcp-verdict-render.test.js
'use strict';
const fs = require('fs');
const os = require('os');
const path = require('path');
const { handlers } = require('../src/mcp-server');

// Scratch tree this suite owns: `project` is what the handler treats as the
// project dir, so every write the tool is allowed to make lands inside it and
// the test can assert on it exactly. (Counting entries in os.tmpdir() itself is
// racy — sibling suites create and delete dirs there in parallel.)
let root; let project;
beforeEach(() => {
  root = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'mcp-render-'));
  project = path.join(root, 'proj');
  fs.mkdirSync(project);
});
afterEach(() => { fs.rmSync(root, { recursive: true, force: true }); });

function record() {
  return {
    schemaVersion: 2, type: 'council-tally',
    meta: { runId: 'r', runType: 'headless', date: 'd', chair: 'deepseek', models: ['gemini', 'gpt'], claudeInCouncil: false },
    findings: [{ id: 'A1', raiser: 'gemini', severity: 'major', tier: 'Confirmed', basis: { a: 2, d: 0, n: 0 },
      confidence: 'solid', tierOverride: null, adjudications: [] }],
    rankings: [], streetCred: [], runStats: [], tierCounts: { Confirmed: 1, Contested: 0, Singleton: 0, Disputed: 0 }, judged: true,
  };
}

test('amicus_verdict render:true with outDir returns md AND writes report.html; stays fenced', async () => {
  const outDir = path.join(project, 'run');
  const res = await handlers.amicus_verdict({ record: record(), decisions: [], render: true, outDir }, project);
  const text = res.content[0].text;
  expect(text).toContain('untrusted_sidecar_output'); // fence preserved
  expect(text).toContain('Council Report');           // md rendering returned
  expect(fs.existsSync(path.join(outDir, 'report.html'))).toBe(true);
});

test('amicus_verdict render:true without outDir returns md, writes nothing', async () => {
  const res = await handlers.amicus_verdict({ record: record(), render: true }, project);
  expect(res.content[0].text).toContain('Council Report');
  expect(fs.readdirSync(project)).toEqual([]); // the only dir it could have written to is untouched
});

test('amicus_verdict outDir escaping the project dir → isError, nothing written', async () => {
  const res = await handlers.amicus_verdict(
    { record: record(), render: true, outDir: path.join('..', 'escaped-report') }, project);
  expect(res.isError).toBe(true);
  expect(res.content[0].text).toMatch(/outDir must resolve to a path inside the project directory/);
  expect(fs.existsSync(path.join(root, 'escaped-report'))).toBe(false);
});

});
```

`src/mcp-server.js` — add `isPathInside` to the existing allowlist import (today: `const { isAllowedProjectRoot } = require('./project-root-allowlist');`) and replace the `amicus_verdict` handler body:

```js
  async amicus_verdict(input, project) {
    try {
      const { buildVerdict } = require('./council/verdict');
      const verdict = buildVerdict(input.record, input.decisions || []);
      if (!input.render) {
        return textResult(fenceSidecarOutput(JSON.stringify(verdict)));
      }
      // v4.1 §4.5c: return the markdown rendering (so Cowork can assemble report.md
      // without Bash) and, when an outDir is given, refresh report.html on disk.
      const { buildReport } = require('./council/report');
      const md = buildReport({ verdict }, { format: 'md' });
      if (input.outDir) {
        // Containment parity with amicus_council_run (mcp-council-run.js): an
        // MCP-supplied outDir must not write outside the project directory.
        const cwd = project || getProjectDir(input.project);
        const outDir = path.resolve(cwd, String(input.outDir));
        if (!isPathInside(outDir, cwd)) {
          return textResult(`outDir must resolve to a path inside the project directory (${cwd}).`, true);
        }
        fs.mkdirSync(outDir, { recursive: true, mode: 0o700 });
        fs.writeFileSync(path.join(outDir, 'report.html'), buildReport({ verdict }, { format: 'html' }), { mode: 0o600 });
      }
      return textResult(fenceSidecarOutput(md));
    } catch (err) { return textResult(`verdict build failed: ${err.message}`, true); }
  },
```

(`fs`, `path` and `getProjectDir` are already module-scope in `src/mcp-server.js` — do NOT re-require them inside the handler. The merged v4.0 handler already wraps its JSON in `fenceSidecarOutput`; the non-render branch above is byte-identical to it, so the v4.0 `amicus_verdict` test in `tests/mcp-server.test.js` stays green.)

## Phase 4 — Skill rewrite (Feature 1)

This phase rewrites the second-opinion skill onto the engine. Order matters: register the already-shipped SEAT-BRIEFS.md in postinstall first (Task 10), then create the fallback doc AND register it in `COUNCIL_FILES` in one commit (Task 11 — registering it any earlier turns the copy-executing postinstall suite red), THEN rewrite SKILL.md (Task 12) so the fallback it points to already exists, then the satellite docs (Task 13). The §4.9 pin re-points land inside the same commits that move the prose — the SKILL.md-keyed rows in Task 12, the `commands/council.md` B42 rows in Task 13 — so the suite is green at every commit; Phase 5's Task 14 is the completeness audit plus a residual sweep. Tasks 11–13 also create the new files' docs-pin tests so the new prose is guarded as it lands.

### Task 10: postinstall `COUNCIL_FILES` — install SEAT-BRIEFS

**Files:**
- Modify: `scripts/postinstall.js:31` (`COUNCIL_FILES`)
- Modify: `tests/scripts/postinstall.test.js:34` (the exact `toEqual` `COUNCIL_FILES` pin, plus the now-stale `'fresh install copies all three council files'` test name)
- Test: `tests/postinstall-council-files.test.js` (Create)

VERIFIED (not Task 0 guesswork): `tests/scripts/postinstall.test.js` is the ONLY suite that pins `COUNCIL_FILES`, and it *also* executes the copy (`installCouncilSkill()` into a temp `$HOME`, asserting every entry lands on disk). `tests/postinstall-skill-source.test.js` covers only the **sidecar** skill and never touches `COUNCIL_FILES`.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing new; the existing `COUNCIL_FILES` loop + per-file `overwrite`/`if-missing` semantics.
- Produces: `COUNCIL_FILES` = SKILL.md (overwrite), COUNCIL-DESIGN.md (overwrite), **SEAT-BRIEFS.md (overwrite — closes the spec §4.8 real gap: it ships in the tarball but was never installed)**, MODEL-NOTES.md (if-missing — unchanged, stays machine-local). `MANUAL-ORCHESTRATION.md` is deliberately **not** registered here: `installCouncilSkill()` copies every entry and `tests/scripts/postinstall.test.js` asserts each one lands on disk, so registering a file that does not exist yet turns that suite red. Its registration ships in **Task 11**, in the same commit that creates the file (suite green after every task). `skills/second-opinion/SEAT-BRIEFS.md` already exists in the repo, so this task is green on its own. Upgrade path: `npm i -g amicus@4.1` overwrites the product-code files in place; accumulated MODEL-NOTES is never touched (spec §8).

### Task 15: Council Review Action v2 — `debate` input + withdrawn-exclusion

**Files:**
- Modify: `.github/workflows/council-review.yml` (V4.0 file — add the `debate` input, forward `--debate`, exclude withdrawn findings from annotations, add the "Withdrawn in debate" collapsible)
- Test: `tests/council-review-debate.test.js` (Create — extend the v4.0 workflow-invariant suite)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Council Review Action v2 (C12); the engine's `--debate` (Task 8) + `findings[].debate` metadata (Task 5); the `neutralize()` sed invariant (must survive byte-identical).
- Produces: `council-review.yml` input `debate` (default `false`) → forwards `--debate` to `amicus council run`; the annotation step excludes findings with `debate.action === 'withdrawn'`; the sticky comment gains a "Withdrawn in debate" collapsible listing them (OQ-6). All model text still routed through `neutralize()`. Nothing downstream depends on this landing (spec OQ-6) — it ships because CI is where debate's bounded-cost rigor pays off.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test** — Create `tests/council-review-debate.test.js` (mirror the v4.0 workflow-invariant harness in `tests/scripts/council-review-workflow.test.js` — read the YAML as text and assert on the job/step text):

```js
// tests/council-review-debate.test.js
'use strict';
const fs = require('fs');
const path = require('path');

const WF = fs.readFileSync(path.join(__dirname, '..', '.github', 'workflows', 'council-review.yml'), 'utf-8');

describe('Council Review Action v2 — debate input (spec §5.2 / OQ-6)', () => {
  test('declares a debate input defaulting to false', () => {
    expect(WF).toMatch(/debate:\s*\n(\s+.*\n)*?\s+default:\s*['"]?false['"]?/);
  });
  test('forwards --debate through a string-safe input read (no loose-equality coercion)', () => {
    // On a plain pull_request event `inputs.*` is empty and GitHub's loose ==
    // coerces null→0 and false→0 — `inputs.debate == 'true'` is the exact trap
    // the label gate already guards against. Same idiom here: format() the
    // input into env:, then compare the string in bash.
    expect(WF).toContain("DEBATE: ${{ format('{0}', inputs.debate) }}");
    expect(WF).toContain('ARGS+=(--debate)');
    expect(WF).not.toMatch(/inputs\.debate\s*==/);
  });
  test('excludes withdrawn findings from check-run annotations', () => {
    expect(WF).toMatch(/debate.*withdrawn|withdrawn.*annotation/is);
  });
  test('sticky comment has a Withdrawn in debate collapsible', () => {
    expect(WF).toContain('Withdrawn in debate');
  });
  test('the neutralize() sed invariant is unchanged (byte-identical guard)', () => {
    // v4.0 pins EXACTLY 10 sed rules across the two model-text shells, 5
    // distinct. A third neutralize() copy, a sixth rule, or a reworded rule
    // breaks tests/scripts/council-review-workflow.test.js — so the withdrawn
    // collapsible must reuse the sticky-comment shell's existing function.
    const sedRules = (WF.match(/-e\s+'s[/|][^']+'/g) || []).map(s => s.replace(/^-e\s+/, ''));
    expect(sedRules.length).toBe(10);
    expect(new Set(sedRules).size).toBe(5);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run test to verify it fails**

Run: `npm test -- tests/council-review-debate.test.js`
Expected: FAIL — no `debate` input / no `--debate` / no "Withdrawn in debate".

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation** — edit `.github/workflows/council-review.yml`:
  - `council-review.yml` has **no `workflow_dispatch` trigger** and no action-inputs block — its only input surface is `on.workflow_call.inputs` (models, chair, critic, fail_on, max_cost, diff_cap_bytes, require_label). Add `debate` there, next to `critic`, in the file's existing boolean-input shape (6-space key, 8-space properties, exactly like `require_label`): `description: Add a Stage-2.5 rebuttal round (defense + re-vote) inside the council run`, `type: boolean`, `default: false`.
  - Read it **string-safely**: `inputs.debate == 'true'` is the loose-equality trap this workflow already documents at the label gate (on a plain `pull_request` event `inputs.*` is empty and GitHub coerces `null`→0 / `false`→0, so a bare comparison is unreliable). Copy the file's own idiom — add `DEBATE: ${{ format('{0}', inputs.debate) }}` to the job `env:` block alongside `MODELS`/`CHAIR`/`CRITIC`, then branch in bash immediately after the existing critic conditional in the `amicus council run` step: `if [ "$DEBATE" = "true" ]; then ARGS+=(--debate); fi` (mirroring `if [ -n "$CRITIC" ]; then ARGS+=(--critic "$CRITIC"); fi`).
  - In the annotation-building step (the `gh api` check-run step), filter out findings whose `.debate.action == "withdrawn"` before mapping to annotations (jq: `[.findings[] | select((.debate.action // "") != "withdrawn")]`).
  - In the sticky-comment step, add a `<details><summary>Withdrawn in debate</summary>…</details>` collapsible listing withdrawn findings, with all text through `neutralize()`.
  - Leave the label gate, fork-secret soft-skip, no-checkout, concurrency group, and `neutralize()` sed rules byte-identical. Specifically: put the withdrawn collapsible **inside the existing sticky-comment shell** and reuse that shell's `neutralize()` — a third copy of the function or a sixth rule breaks `tests/scripts/council-review-workflow.test.js`, which pins the file to exactly 10 sed rules / 5 distinct.

- [ ] **Step 4: Run test + actionlint**

Run: `npm test -- tests/council-review-debate.test.js tests/scripts/council-review-workflow.test.js` (the v4.0 invariant suite lives under `tests/scripts/`, not `tests/`)
Expected: PASS — debate assertions green; the v4.0 invariants (label gate, neutralize harvest, conclusion mapping) still green.

Run actionlint (pinned-binary, B43 pattern). There is **no** `lint:actions` npm script — `ci.yml:48-51` downloads the pinned v1.7.7 binary on the runner, and the local precedent is to run that same pinned binary by hand before a workflow change lands:

```powershell
$dir = Join-Path $env:TEMP 'actionlint-1.7.7'
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force $dir | Out-Null
Invoke-WebRequest -Uri 'https://github.com/rhysd/actionlint/releases/download/v1.7.7/actionlint_1.7.7_windows_amd64.zip' -OutFile "$dir\actionlint.zip"
Expand-Archive -Force "$dir\actionlint.zip" $dir
# run from the repo root so a .github/actionlint.yaml would be honored
& "$dir\actionlint.exe" -color .github/workflows/council-review.yml
```

Expected: exit 0, no findings. Without a local `shellcheck.exe` on PATH actionlint skips the shellcheck pass — acceptable, since CI's ubuntu `quality` job re-runs actionlint **with** shellcheck. If a finding would force an edit to a byte-pinned line, add a narrowly-scoped, per-rule-documented `.github/actionlint.yaml` instead of rewording the pin (`ci.yml`'s stated policy).

```bash
git commit -m "feat(ci): Council Review Action v2 debate input + withdrawn-exclusion (OQ-6)" -m "Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>"
```

**Phase 5 complete:** docs suites green, CI exposes debate.

- **OQ-1** (extract manual) → Tasks 11/12; **OQ-2** (`--claude-review` engine input) → Task 7; **OQ-3** (red-team → `--critic`) → Ambiguity 14 + Task 12; **OQ-4** (retire matrix/verdict.md in fast path) → Ambiguity 12 + Task 12; **OQ-5** (4-item menu, verdict scale standard) → Ambiguity 13 + Task 12; **OQ-6** (Action v2 debate input) → Task 15; **OQ-7** (v4.1.0 minor, overwrite) → Ambiguity 16 + Task 16.

**Coverage gaps:** none. The four items an adversarial review flagged are all closed with real code + tests: (1) §4.4 chair-packet Claude inclusion — Task 7 Step 3 appends `{model:'claude', text}` to the chair-packet `reviews` array, asserted by the `Review by claude` check in Task 7 Step 1; (2) §5.7 debate-degradation → exit 2 — `runDebate` returns `degraded`/`aborted`, run.js wires both (Task 4 Step 5), asserted across Task 4 Step 7's matrix; (3) §6 driver degradation matrix — the dead-defense/partial-and-fully-dead-re-vote/both-cost-ceiling/abort/lenses-no-ledger cases are written out in full in Task 4 Step 7; (4) the fake-launcher helpers (`defenseOut`/`revoteOut`/`launchersFromScript`/`happyScriptMap`/`happyLaunchers`/`debateScriptMap`/`debateScript`) are written as complete code in Task 4 Step 6, and Task 0 Step 2 verifies the v4.0 helper exports they build on. The one item deliberately NOT a code task is the "always fresh" Claude-review rule (spec §4.4 — engine cannot verify freshness; it stays a SKILL.md rule, pinned by Task 12's Claude-in-council prose). The 1-model scale-down stays a manual SKILL.md path (Task 12), not an engine feature (spec §2 non-goal).

**Placeholder scan:** no `TBD`/`implement later`/`similar to Task N`/prose-without-code. Every code step shows real code and every command shows an expected result. The fake-launcher helpers Tasks 4/7 import (`debateScript`, `happyLaunchers`, `defenseOut`, `revoteOut`, `launchersFromScript`, `happyScriptMap`, `debateScriptMap`) are written as complete bodies in Task 4 Step 6 (not prose), and Task 0 Step 2 verifies the v4.0 exports (`scriptedLaunchers`/`happyScript`/`review`/`judgeOut`/`mkLeg`/`okWave`/`baseOptions`) they reuse. No forward-dependency handoff leaves the suite red: Tasks 12 and 13 re-point their own §4.9 doc pins inside the commits that move the prose, and Task 14 is the §4.9 completeness audit plus residual sweep — the ordering is stated in all three tasks. One anchor is recorded-not-asserted per the plan's Task-0 discipline: the lenses+debate test (Task 4 Step 7) uses the `<runId>-l1..lN` per-seat lens wave scheme that Task 0 Step 3 records, and instructs the executor to adapt the keys if merged v4.0 differs — the *behavior* under test (lens runs never append to the ledger) is fully coded and asserted regardless of the waveId spelling.

**Type consistency:** the debate parser result shape (`{byId, ok, errors}`), the `debate.json` shape, the `run.json` `debate` summary, and the `findings[].debate` decoration are defined once in Shared contracts and used byte-consistently in Tasks 1 (parsers) → 3 (reassembly/decoration) → 4 (orchestration/summary) → 5 (schemas/verdict carry) → 6 (renderer view) → 12/15 (consumers). The present-tense→past-tense action mapping is single-sourced in `debate.js` `PAST_TENSE` (Task 3) and reused by Task 4's addendum outcomes and Task 6's renderer. `runCouncil` options (`debate`/`claudeReviewFile`/`noCostGate`) are declared once (Shared contracts) and threaded identically through the CLI handler (Task 8), MCP handler (Task 8), and run-launch forwarding (Task 8) into the debate launches (Task 4). The `--render` contract (write-only-with-outDir) is stated once (Ambiguity 15 + Shared contracts) and honored identically in the CLI (Task 9) and MCP (Task 9) handlers.

## Execution handoff

Plan complete and saved to `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-19-v4.1-skill-on-engine.md`. Two execution options:

**1. Subagent-Driven (recommended)** — superpowers:subagent-driven-development: fresh subagent per task, review between tasks (repo convention; Task 0 first; Task 16 Steps 1–3 and 8 are USER-GATED — real spend and push/tag need Christian's explicit go).

**2. Inline Execution** — superpowers:executing-plans: batch execution with checkpoints.

Phase 1 (Tasks 0–6) is independently shippable (debate engine, no skill change). Feature 1 (Tasks 7–14) depends on Phase 1 only for the `findings[].debate` metadata Stage 4 reads; both ride the single v4.1.0 release.
